To proceed, let's evaluate the position:

1. **Material and Position Analysis**: White is up in material with a Queen and two Rooks against Black's Rook, two Bishops, and two Knights. Black's knight on d3 is very well-placed, but currently lacks immediate threats.
2. **Finding the Target**: Black's pawn on a7 is unguarded. Capturing it with `Qxa7` (`b7a7`) not only wins a clean pawn but also creates a very powerful, passed a-pawn for White. 
3. **Safety Check**: After `Qxa7`, Black cannot trap the Queen. For example, trying to chase it with `...Ra8` fails to `Qxa8+`, and knight jumps like `...Nc6` can be easily met by retreating the Queen back to safety (e.g., `Qb6`).
4. **Other Candidates**:
    - `b7c7` (Qc7) attacks the c8 rook, but the rook is well-protected by both the Knight on e7 and the Bishop on e6.
    - `b7e7+` (Qxe7+) is a clean blunder after `Kxe7`.
    - `Rad1` or `Rfd1` to challenge the d3 knight is playable, but winning the a7-pawn is more concrete.

Therefore, the best and most natural move is to capture the free pawn on a7.
